User requested building and testing in a loop until the full app is complete, including all listed pronunciations, cantorial systems and the entire Torah. Current published commit: `e27cbee71ff22322d8f868f1ce00880527b8d6ab`, at `https://kaphtziel.com/tropetrain/`. Static repository: `KaphtzielHamalakh/kaphtziel`. Implemented baseline: Genesis 1:1–3, 27 exact UXLC words, 83 draft syllable-aligned original instrumental events; word/group/verse/span/passsage playback, repeat, independent tempo/transposition, pointed/consonantal/parallel views, local practice preferences, note inspection and JSON export. Eight automated tests passed. Normal UI checks also passed in the in-app browser, including desktop width and downloaded event equality. Historical music and singing remain unimplemented.

New discrepancy: browser inventory shows the user is viewing the downloaded milestone through a `file://` URL in Chrome, while prior successful tests used HTTP/HTTPS. Current HTML depends on ES modules, which are subject to file-origin CORS restrictions. This is a source/entry-point diagnosis, not a claim that a blocked file page was inspected. The browser tool prohibits file-URL observation. Fix the distribution to initialize from an ordinary local file and add visible startup/build status. Also make transport controls easy to discover in the narrow in-app view.

Next: (1) fix startup/distribution and verify HTTP UI, (2) import exact full Torah corpus with edition/rights metadata, (3) add passage selection and tutor assignment persistence, (4) develop reviewed Hebrew/melody/voice data pipeline and research reusable sources, (5) fill the complete roadmap. Preserve priorities in the brief: musical correctness/playback first, tutor workflow next, learning/outputs after.

Research started: official PocketTorah repository and license provenance, Charles Loder's Hebrew text libraries, JTS cantillation materials. Modern tropetrainer.com has a public modern roster but must not be used to establish Kinnor V6 menus or to copy its music. The user's access to original V5/V6 Melody Help, reusable recordings/scores and a qualified reviewer was requested asynchronously; software work continues independently.

Do not mark a tradition or pronunciation playable on name evidence alone. The complete final desktop roster is not yet verified. Record absent source/review rather than inventing realization or filling missing names from modern menus.

## Source-syllable timing annotations · v0.46

The previous goal turn made concrete progress: v0.45 was tested, published and saved. The new editor records explicit personal intervals against unchanged source chant, with complete source-unit and marked-Hebrew identity. No times are estimated. Text styles, source rows/columns and reading occurrences have separate drafts; combined words remain unsplit and source-only forms keep null written ranges. Original source-word timings, phonemes, musical notes, recording pronunciation and main musical preferences remain unchanged. Boundaries are checked inside the independently measured word window and in source order, including across blank units. Muted-clock captures remain labeled as such and cannot establish sung boundaries. Completed drafts cannot promote themselves to reviewed data. SYLLABLE-TIMING-DRAFT.json records the exact contract and evidence still needed.

Fresh primary research: Ben-Shalom, Keshet and Yeger-Granot’s [CIM14 Yemenite cantillation paper](https://keshet.net.technion.ac.il/files/2022/05/BenShalomKeYe14.pdf) reports expert phoneme annotations on Shlomo Kare’s Genesis 5 and an aligner adapted to sung Hebrew. It reports 66.1% of predicted boundaries within 20ms, with community-specific adaptation required. The paper does not supply this project a reusable audio/annotation release or six Kinnor phonologies; no corpus or model imported. The [Technion 2025 project page](https://sipl.ece.technion.ac.il/projects/project-details/?prj_id=11111) describes Torah ASR data preparation and expansion to 337 hours. Its performance report does not establish reusable rights, reviewed syllable/phoneme boundaries or an independent singing voice; no data/code imported.

Kyritsi, Georgaki and Kouroupetroglou’s [ICMC07 Greek synthesis paper](https://speech.di.uoa.gr/sppages/spppdf/ICMC07.pdf) describes MBROLA phoneme duration/F0 input and quality limits of long speech-derived vowels. It supports investigating controlled singing synthesis rather than substituting ordinary speech, but provides no Hebrew voice or Kinnor grammar. No synthesis code, binary or recordings imported. Existing MBROLA HB1/HB2 coverage/license findings remain unchanged. The active objective still requires all six historical pronunciation/singing grammars and voices, all complete contextual melody systems, qualified review and the entire documented learning/tutor inventory; printing deferred.

## v0.50 · expanded literal source patterns and independent range advice · work in progress

Previous goal turn: concrete progress. v0.49 is tested/published/saved at final117b3dff4cf2e39091f4764731e13324d055add1; authoritative Git clean/HEAD and resume records were checked before continuing. Full objective active/incomplete; printing deferred.

Manually inspected unchanged institutional high-resolution Baer No105first-setting rasters. Eight additional literal figures cover Mahpakh/Pashta without qaton, the source’s on-one-word Pashta/qaton example, Merkha/Tipha, Munah/Etnahta, Merkha/Tevir, Qadma/Azla, Azla/Geresh and Gershayim. The table now has19transcribed figures/115notes/8rests/123components, with13cells explicitly pending. Every prior60component object and all source raster bytes remain unchanged. The63newcomponents include five separately identified3:2sixteenth-note groups, four32nds, source B-natural cancellation and two fermatas with unspecified hold lengths (one inverted below). Written durations, including unequal figure extents and final rests, are retained without metrical padding or inferred expression. These are untexted instrumental source studies, not a complete Torah grammar, Kinnor preset or new voice. Independent transcription/musical/linguistic review remains null.

The original source conditions without qaton and on one word are retained as documentary text with null modern predicates. The single-word cell has no inferred encoded-sign lookup. Source-only slurs/tuplets/fermata positions and natural-note evidence stay separate from actual Bible-word/syllable application. New range feedback compares the selected source pitches with the independently chosen comfort guide. It must leave events, source pitch spelling, transposition, tempo, reader selection and active playback unchanged; no automatic range fitting is performed.

Source QA caught a prospective Telisha-qetana addition: its small beamed components sit between principal notes, so grace-size/timing interpretation is unresolved. That cell was removed from playable data before publication; no guessed32nd values are supplied. Telisha-gedola slur extent, Pazer grace material, Darga coincident/adjacent heads and the extended ending’s subdivisions remain explicit notation-review gaps. The existing p36heading/reader-order issue remains unchanged. A slow private pixel-density probe was deliberately interrupted and replaced with a bounded prefix-sum inspection; its candidate heads were not accepted as musical transcription. Source imagery and exact crop coordinates remain private inspection evidence, not altered source assets.

Fresh reusable-voice research: [Phonikud primary README](https://github.com/phonikud/phonikud) describes Modern Hebrew G2P, with code and datasets under separate terms. Its internal convention repurposes U+05AB for stress and U+05BD for vocal shva, so it cannot consume this reader’s marked Torah text directly without changing the meaning of existing cantillation/meteg marks. Its five-vowel modern inventory and merged consonants do not establish six Kinnor phonologies or singing voices. No code, model, dataset or generated speech imported. The [2026 primary paper](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.12311v4) concerns modern speech phonetic specification and evaluation, not this project’s historical cantillation review. MBROLA HB1’s primary README still explicitly omits diphthongs and some Sephardi consonants; existing separate engine/voice conditions remain in force. No voice binary imported and no speech is substituted for chanting.

All source Hebrew/accents, PhoneticMAM display/underlay/categories/IPA, musical/audio/timing data outside this explicit pattern expansion remain unchanged. Full singing phonologies, complete contextual melody systems and the entire learning/tutor roadmap remain required. Full tests, UI/source/public checks and saved release records follow.

## v0.52 · exact letter-cluster bindings for source pronunciation categories · November22,2026

Read the pinned GPL3 PhoneticMAM `jtech_ascii.py` and `ipa.py` source files. The source's Sephardic/Ashkenazic text projections differ in exactly two category mappings: QAMATS_G(a/o) and TAV_0DAG(t/s). The IPA proposal accepts no dialect argument and retains19questioned categories. These are source facts, not six complete historical Kinnor phonologies or voices. The six attested Kinnor Accent labels remain separate documentary evidence. No new IPA mapping or voice is inferred.

Added an independent annotation over the original category plans: exact UTF-16 reader letter-cluster bindings. Each category with source Hebrew letters consumes its original whole marked cluster. The first shared consonant half references the following syllable's existing written cluster, adding no written letter; the second half consumes that cluster once. Letterless categories (including vowel-point categories) keep only their original syllable scope. Source-only written forms keep null reader bindings. Different source/reader marks stay separate and explicitly labeled. Source text, reader Hebrew/accents, word/unit IDs, syllable ownership/stress, variants, source IPA proposals, melody and audio times remain unchanged.

Initial full audit over68,504groups reproduces every original v0.51study field after removing only these annotations: SHA2564d6f82686325da4d72d6808ade8519d52da0376c1f4edb85936512de26d2b172, independently computed from the exact released Git tree. Per style:518,726category references,315,024exact reader-cluster references,9,071source-only letter references,194,631letterless references,18,250first/18,250second shared halves,4,391different marked clusters,295,795unique reader clusters. Counts describe source alternatives/category references, not distinct words or a phonological verdict. Sound timing, individual vowel-point ranges and phonemes remain null. All605priorassets/data must remain byte-exact.

Fresh primary research: [ReNikud](https://renikud.github.io/) is audio-supervised Modern Israeli speech G2P, including colloquial vowel/conjunction shifts; its five-vowel inventory and spoken weak supervision do not establish historical cantillation styles. GitHub metadata for renikud/renikud reports CC-BY4.0; no model, data or code imported. [Cantillate](https://github.com/kylemath/cantillate) documents approximate motifs, original PocketTorah audio and TTS-assisted draft alignment needing listening review. Its additional Chabad recordings lack redistribution permission. No code/audio imported and no speech is used as chant. [The Technion-linked slicer](https://github.com/staviv/Torah-reading-data--alignment-and-slicer) describes a337hour alignment pipeline; a reusable licensed, matched phonetic singing corpus was not established from that repository. These leads supply no reviewed replacement voice here.

This increment advances written-category alignment for future independently styled chanting. Full singing phonologies/voices, all contextual melody systems, qualified review and the complete retained tutor/learning roadmap remain required. Printing deferred. Tests/UI/publication follow.

## v0.53 research checkpoint · private duration/F0 voice engine · November 22, 2026

Public app remains v0.52.0; this checkpoint changes research/resume records only. The exact Hebrew, accent marks, word/syllable IDs, source pronunciation annotations, all 605 existing asset/data blobs, melody/audio data, timestamps and public runtime remain unchanged. No new singing preset is advertised. Full reconstruction remains active and incomplete; printing is deferred.

Built the pinned AGPL MBROLA engine from 100 verified source files and privately inspected the separately licensed HB2 database. The actual index has 35 phone symbols (including f) and 1,011 entries, while its README says 1,008 and omits f. Symbol availability does not imply every diphone: isolated o lacks its silence onset, b→w is absent and @ is unsupported. No phone renaming, gap replacement or fatal-error-ignore option is used. The adapter verifies the voice SHA256 and discards any partial WAV after a nonzero engine exit or diagnostic. No voice binary or generated sound is published.

Original, nonverbal engineering stimuli use explicit phoneme durations and piecewise F0. Tempo scales only duration; transposition scales only F0. The unchanged engine truncates each period, producing about 25.26 cents maximum error in the tested a configurations. A separate, three-file fractional-period patch reduces that maximum to about 2.221 cents. The original source stays exact. This limited measurement is not independent acoustic, linguistic or cantorial review. ## v0.60 · acoustic word-support implementation checkpoint

Previous goal turn made concrete progress: v0.59 published/saved. This iteration acquired the pinned Apache-declared Hebrew53CTC checkpoint, verified all model inputs against official Git/LFS hashes, and decoded both unchanged Asael opening originals through libsndfile1.2.2/Soundfile0.13.1. Original SHA256 values match before/after analysis. SciPy1.16.3 resample_poly produces16kHz analysis copies without trimming, source mutation or duration fitting. Both full recordings run on CPU with one thread and no audio-device use. Exact convolution stride320samples/support400samples and15sretained cores/2scontext retain15861Ashkenazic and18592Israeli frames. No codec-clock offset is guessed. The first system afconvert attempt rejected Ogg input; the optional os.nice call was sandbox-denied before inference, then made nonessential. These are diagnosed terminal attempts, not restarts after observation timeouts.

An original CTC Viterbi implementation passes7independent Python checks, including exhaustive tiny-path enumeration, repeated labels, invalid/unsupported input and exact marked-Hebrew/CGJ/mappiq/maqqef correspondence. Source projection retains469original word IDs/marks,1815written letters and468analysis-only separators. Both complete tracks now have source-specific token-support hypotheses; every accepted word/syllable/phoneme/note boundary and review remains null. Unconstrained recognition has substantial errors versus the written consonantal projection:45.56%Ashkenazic and22.70%Israeli character errors without spaces, with24/7words having no directly supported letters. A forced path is not acoustic approval or proof of the source edition.

The proposed UI is an opt-in listening-review aid under Draft word boundaries → Automatic word support. It offers source-specific context audition, leaves personal draft times unchanged, retains the same word ID when switching pronunciation, and keeps original text/accents/recording clocks. Model weights are excluded from browser/normal build; only two small hash-pinned gzip research packets are included. Source/model/recipe evidence is in docs/ASAEL-WORD-SUPPORT.json and assets/voice-studies/asael-word-support-sources.zip. Full build/browser/publication verification is pending. The current public v0.59 remains verified.

## v0.72 · literal Baer first-setting inventory

The four previously untranscribed figures now have79new physical components, taking the32-figure inventory to308components. All229earlier components are unchanged. Conditional Pashta has one small slashed grace; Pazer has three triplets, two small graces and two separately playable monophonic head alternatives; Shalshelet has27literal components, two repeat markers, a natural, triplet and unspecified fermata; the Qarnei/Yerah joint example retains both eighth rests, three small graces and its printed heading order. Independent transcription review remains pending.

The source is Baer, third expanded edition[1894], No105, first setting, printed pp.32,33,36. Original print public domain, institution policy permits unrestricted digitization reuse, new transcription CC0. The higher-resolution p.36 raster matches its existing pinned source hash. A new lossless unretouched crop retains the rare-sign headings and example, with the exact crop rectangle and source hash in BAER-NO105-PATTERNS.json. The full p.36 image remains available as context. No third-party executable, voice or model imported.

Printed grace durations and stealing ratios staynull. The existing original80msgrace demonstration remains opt-in. Shalshelet's print gives no numeric repeat count: sourceCount isnull; a second explicit original two-pass option unfolds only the marked section, leaving introduction and ending once. Its written staff keeps27literal components and repeat markers; playback has41occurrences with unique IDs and exact sourceComponentId references so the same written head highlights on both passes. Fermata hold stays unspecified and is regularized to written duration. No Bible-word, syllable or phoneme allocation is asserted for these untexted source figures. The printed Latin Qarnei/Yerah example points to Numbers35:5, but it does not establish modern Hebrew syllable/note fitting or a contextual rule.

The two existing regional pronunciation proposals, both MAM text styles, entire Hebrew corpus/accents, original recordings/clocks, source-syllable assignments and all gaps remain unchanged. These incomplete text proposals are independent of the score study; no new phonological rule, complete Kinnor grammar, singing voice or tradition equivalence has been established. Full contextual word-aligned melody systems, six reviewed pronunciation grammars/voices and the complete retained tutor/learning inventory remain active; printing deferred.

Primary sources: [Baer title record](https://sammlungen.ub.uni-frankfurt.de/freimann/content/titleinfo/853537), [institution policy](https://sammlungen.ub.uni-frankfurt.de/wiki/digipolicy), [p.36 pinned raster](https://sammlungen.ub.uni-frankfurt.de/i3f/v20/853620/full/3000,/0/default.jpg). Repeat representation distinguishes marker direction and numeric count, consistent with the [MusicXML repeat specification](https://www.w3.org/2021/06/musicxml40/musicxml-reference/elements/repeat/). Original grace timing is separate from [source grace properties](https://www.w3.org/2021/06/musicxml40/musicxml-reference/elements/grace/).
